C H A P T E R 6
Guide for Certification of Bow, Stern and Sideport Ramps and Moveable
Platforms (Decks)
S E C T I O N 5
Maintenance
1 Repairs
When important repairs or renewals are required to be made to the lifting devices of ramps, moveable
platforms (decks) and portable ramps, the repairs are to be carried out under the attendance and to the
satisfaction of the Surveyor. Tests and examination of the particular lifting devices as may be deemed
necessary are to be carried out in accordance with [6-3/5] . Certificates covering tests are to be attached to
Register of Lifting Appliances (see Section [2-8] ).
When welding is used to lengthen, alter or repair chains, rings, links, shackles or swivels, they are to be
properly heat treated and are to be adequately tested and examined in accordance with [6-3/2] and certificate
furnished before being again put in use. The certificates are to be attached to the Register of Lifting Appliances
(see Section [2-8] ).
3 Addition of New Gear and Wire Rope
When articles of loose gear and wire rope conforming with tests in accordance with [6-3/2] and [6-3/3]
are supplied from time to time, the vessel’s officer designated by the Master is to enter and initial such
replacements in the record noted in Section [2-8] kept with the Register of Lifting Appliances (See Section
[2-8] ), identifying each article and certificate of same.
5 Splicing of Wire Rope
A thimble or loop splice made in any wire rope is to have at least three (3) tucks with a whole strand of the
rope and two (2) tucks with one-half of the wires cut out of each strand, provided that this requirement does
not prevent the use of another form of splice which can be shown to be as efficient as that required in this
Section. Clips for splicing wire rope are not acceptable.
7 Condition of Wire Rope
No wire rope is to be used if in any length of eight (8) diameters, the total number of visible broken wires
exceeds 10% of the total number of wires, or if the rope shows signs of excessive wear, corrosion or other
defect which renders it unfit for use.
ABS REQUIREMENTS FOR CERTIFICATION OF LIFTING APPLIANCES • 2026 219

<!-- PDF page 226 -->

Chapter 6 Guide for Certification of Bow, Stern and Sideport Ramps and Moveable Platforms
(Decks)
Section 5 Maintenance 6-5
9 Knots in Chain
Chains are not to be shortened by tying knots in them.
11 Annealing of Chains and Connecting Elements
Chains, rings, links, shackles and swivels of wrought iron used in lifting operations are to be annealed at
the following intervals.
Chains and gear in general use and of 12.7 mm (0.5 in.) or less, once at least in every six months.
All other chains and gear in general use, once at least every 12 months.
13 Annealing Details
The annealing is to be done in suitable closed oven and not over an open fire. Wrought iron is to be annealed
at a temperature of between 593°C to 649°C (1100°F to 1200°F) for a period between 30 and 60 minutes.
After being annealed, the article should be allowed to cool slowly.
15 Annealing Certificate
A certificate on prescribed form (see Form CHG-6 in [A1-1] ) is to be furnished by the firm undertaking
the annealing, describing gear annealed, which is to be attached to the Register of Lifting Appliances (see
Section [2-8] ).
ABS REQUIREMENTS FOR CERTIFICATION OF LIFTING APPLIANCES • 2026 220

C H A P T E R 7
Personnel Transfer Appliances
S E C T I O N 1
Rigid Personnel Transfer Baskets (1 February 2025)
1 General
Rigid personnel transfer baskets are devices used to transfer personnel from one vessel to another, over
water, and lifted by a crane certified for personnel lifting. The personnel transfers may be from an offshore
support vessel to an offshore platform, or between two vessels transferring personnel as part of their lightering
operation. These rigid personnel transfer baskets or carriers can be designed in the form of baskets, capsules,
or cages. These baskets can be design assessed (PDA), or unit certified in accordance with Appendix 1A-1-
A4 of ABS Rules for Conditions of Classification (Part 1A).
1.1 Objective
1.1.1 Goals
The rigid personnel transfer baskets addressed in this section are to be designed, constructed,
operated and tested to:
Goal No. Goal
OTH SG23 Provide means for the safe lifting, handling or transfer of personnel.
OTH SG25 Prevent failures or accidents, which would cause harm to personnel or damage to ships,
offshore facilities, subsea or shore-based structures, as well as the environment.
SAFE 1 Promote the occupational health and safety of personnel onboard.
SAFE 1.2 Provide means to minimize the risk of strikes against objects/equipment, slips, trips, and falls
within the vessel and overboard.
Materials are to be suitable for the intended application in accordance with the following goal in
support of the Tier 1 goals as listed above.
ABS REQUIREMENTS FOR CERTIFICATION OF LIFTING APPLIANCES • 2026 222

<!-- PDF page 229 -->

Chapter 7 Personnel Transfer Appliances
Section 1 Rigid Personnel Transfer Baskets 7-1
Goal No. Goal
MAT 1 The selected materials’ physical, mechanical and chemical properties are to meet the design
requirements appropriate for the application, operating conditions and environment.
The goals covered in the cross-referenced sections of this document are also to be met.
1.1.2 Functional Requirements
In order to achieve the above stated goals, the design, construction, operation and testing of the rigid
personnel transfer basket are to meet the following functional requirements:
FR No. Functional Requirements
OTH-FR1 (SAFE) Load bearing members are to have sufficient strength and integrity, when the basket is
operated within its design, operational and environmental parameters.
OTH-FR2 (MAT) Materials used are to have corrosion and wear resistance suitable for the operating
environment; or are to be provided with means of corrosion control.
OTH-FR3 (SAFE) Designed to accommodate the required number of personnel and their luggage in fully
upright or seated position, with appropriate means of securing and holding.
OTH-FR4 Baskets are to be marked with essential information such as rating and certification standard
to allow proper operation.
OTH-FR5 (SAFE) Designed to protect personnel from injuries due to contact with ship structures, falling
objects, or from slips, trips or falls.
OTH-FR6 (SAFE) Provided with means for crew to manually control basket movement and reduce risk of side
impacts.
OTH-FR7 Designed with redundancy to withstand failure of a single component (e.g. cables, wire
ropes, slings, shackles, hooks, or rings) in the main lifting set.
OTH-FR8 (SAFE) Designed to have stable and cushioned transfer during operation considering its design,
operational and environmental parameters.
OTH-FR9 (SAFE) Designed to prevent lifting set or top of basket from hitting personnel during the transfer.
OTH-FR10 Provided with means to remain afloat within its design, operation, and environmental
(SAFE) parameters.
OTH-FR11 Prevent accumulation of water on deck or in components to protect against slips, corrosion
(SAFE/MAT) and premature failure.
OTH-FR12 Provide adequate number and size of openings in the basket for entry and egress considering
(SAFE) the number of personnel carried.
The functional requirements covered in the cross-referenced sections of this document are also to
be met.
1.1.3 Compliance
Rigid personnel transfer baskets are considered to comply with the Goals and Functional
Requirements within their scope of certification when the applicable prescriptive requirements are
complied with, or when an alternative arrangement has been approved, refer to Part 1D, Chapter 2.
ABS REQUIREMENTS FOR CERTIFICATION OF LIFTING APPLIANCES • 2026 223

<!-- PDF page 230 -->

Chapter 7 Personnel Transfer Appliances
Section 1 Rigid Personnel Transfer Baskets 7-1
3 References (1 April 2025)
Designers or builders may refer to the following publications for additional guidance.
i) Brazil Marine Directorate of Ports and Coasts (DPC) - Maritime Authority Regulations for Material
Approval - NORMAM-05/DPC, 4-4 and Annex 4-A - Standards for Personnel Transfer Basket
ii) NORSOK Standard R-002 - Lifting Equipment, Annex A.8
iii) BS EN 14502-1 - Cranes - Equipment for the lifting of persons - Part 1: Suspended Baskets
iv) The American Society of Mechanical Engineers (ASME) B30.23 - Personnel Lifting Systems
v) United States Department of Labor - Occupational Safety and Health Administration (OSHA) - 29
C.F.R. 1926.1431 - Hoisting Personnel
vi) Oil Companies International Marine Forum (OCIMF) - Transfer of Personnel by Crane between
Vessels
5 Definitions
The definitions in 2-1/7 apply to this Chapter in addition to the ones stated below.
i) Lifting Set
A term used to refer to dedicated loose gear items used to connect the personnel lifting crane and the
basket structure, i.e., between a crane hook and lifting lugs fixed to the top of the basket structure.
ii) Safe Working Load (SWL) of the Basket
Maximum load (including personnel weight and other payload) that the basket is approved to carry.
In some references, this may be stated as rated capacity or working load limit.
iii) Gross Weight (Fully Loaded Basket)
The gross weight of a fully loaded basket includes its own weight, weight of its associated lifting
set and accessories, and the SWL.
Commentary:
This weight is used to evaluate the suitability of the personnel lifting crane to lift the basket and is not to exceed
the personnel SWL rating of the crane.
End of Commentary
ABS REQUIREMENTS FOR CERTIFICATION OF LIFTING APPLIANCES • 2026 224

7 Plans and information to be submitted
The following plans and information are to be submitted.
(R): Documents to be reviewed
(I): Documents for information and verification for consistency with related review
(OB): Documentation to be kept on board
i) Lifting arrangement drawing showing the following information:
● maximum weight of the fully loaded basket (R),

Chapter 7 Personnel Transfer Appliances
Section 1 Rigid Personnel Transfer Baskets 7-1
● general arrangement showing the dimensions and geometry of the load, including location of
center of gravity and lifting set assembly (R),
● calculations of the resultant working loads on each component of the lifting set (R),
● material specifications (I), and
● applicable certificates and standards of the components (I).
● sample manufacturer’s certificate for each basket (I)
ii) Basket design and construction drawings and calculations showing the following information:

iv) Operation and Maintenance Manual (I, OB)
9 Materials, Welding and Non-Destructive Inspection
i) Where structural members are made of steel, materials, welding and NDE are to meet the applicable
requirements of Section 2-3. Materials other than steel used for structural members such as aluminum
and fiber-reinforced plastics are subject to ABS design assessment and approval based on ABS Rules
for Materials and Welding (Part 2).
ii) Components are to be fabricated from or coated by corrosion resistant materials to resist the corrosive
effects of salt water.
11 Lifting Set including Loose Gear Items and Wire Ropes
i) Wire ropes are to meet the applicable requirements of Section 2-4, and are to have a Safe Working
Load (SWL) of not less than the maximum load in each rope considering a lifting load of 2 x (Basket
SWL + Weight of Empty Basket) for the lifting set.
ii) Loose gear items (e.g., links, shackles, slings) are to meet the applicable requirements of Section
2-5, and are to have a Safe Working Load (SWL) of not less than the maximum load in each rope
considering a lifting load of 2 x (Basket SWL + Weight of Empty Basket) for the lifting set.
iii) For a fully loaded basket, the weights of each person and each person’s luggage are to be at least
90 kg (198 lb) and 20 kg (44 lb) respectively.
iv) The length of the lifting set is to allow sufficient slack after lowering to avoid contact between crane
hook and top of the basket structure. The vertical distance between the crane hook and the lifting
lug(s) on the basket is to be greater than the significant wave heights of design sea state limit.
Commentary:
For example, if sea state 5 is the stated operating limit, then the vertical distance should be at least 4 m (13.3 ft).
End of commentary
ABS REQUIREMENTS FOR CERTIFICATION OF LIFTING APPLIANCES • 2026 227

<!-- PDF page 234 -->

Chapter 7 Personnel Transfer Appliances
Section 1 Rigid Personnel Transfer Baskets 7-1
v) The design of the lifting set is to prevent snagging with the top of the basket structure.
vi) Disconnectable components such as shackles are to be provided with locking arrangements to
prevent accidental opening.
vii) The lifting set is to be dedicated to the rigid personnel transfer basket and is not to be used for other
purposes such as cargo or equipment lifting. The lifting set is to be connected to the basket structure
using methods such as shackles with bolt, nut and cotter pins that require specific tools for release.
viii) A second lifting set including links connecting to the crane hook and lugs on the basket structure
is to be provided to handle the loads upon loss of the first lifting set. This can be in the form of a
duplicate of the main lifting set or a single safety cable. This second lifting set is also to meet the
same requirements as main lifting set except that the second lifting set is to be rated for the basket
SWL plus the weight of the empty basket instead the weights specified under Paragraphs 11(i) and
(ii) above, and proof loads are to be that specified under 2-5/2.1. If the second lifting set is not loaded
during normal operations, length of the second lifting set is to be only minimally longer (e.g. 100
mm (4 in.)) than the main lifting set to prevent excessive shock when taking over from the latter.
13 Basket Structure
i) The design calculations for the basket structure are to be based on a lifting load of not less than
2 x (Basket SWL + Weight of Empty Basket), and are to consider other applicable loads (such as
dynamic loads due to motion and impact loads), as specified by the designer.
a) The allowable stresses for steel rigid structural members of the basket structure are to be
calculated as per 2-2/5.5.
b) The allowable stresses for aluminum are to comply with standards or codes such as the
Aluminum Design Manual, 2020 and are subject to ABS design assessment and approval.
ii) For loose gear items, wire ropes and slings that are used for basket structure, refer to 7-1/11.
Refer to 7-1/11.iii for weight requirements to calculate SWL. Refer to 13.v below for impact load
requirements.
iii) It is to be designed with at least two accesses each having a minimum width of at least 600 mm
(24 in.) each. For baskets that are provided with one access/egress per person carried, the minimum
width may be reduced to 500 mm (20 in.). Gates or doors, which may hinder egress, are not to be
installed at the access locations.
iv) It is to be of rigid structure that retains its form when the lifting set is slack. This may be achieved
with a central column or with columns at the sides of the basket. In this regard, a flexible basket that
can collapse during operation is not acceptable.
v) Devices for absorbing impact loads are to be provided to reduce harmful accelerations/decelerations
when the basket is subjected to vertical impact at raising or lowering, or horizontal impact due
to swinging against a structure. The basket is to withstand a vertical impact of 1 m/s (2.22 mph)
and a horizontal impact of 1 m/s (2.22 mph) without affecting its load carrying capability. In this
regard, load bearing structures are to be designed to withstand the normal operational loads and the
additional impact loads. The horizontal and vertical impact loads need not be applied simultaneously.
vi) The dimensional sizing of the basket is to be based on the maximum number of personnel and
luggage that it is expected to accommodate. Personnel may be either in standing position or seated
position during transfer. Standing positions are to have free-standing height of at least 2 m (6.56 ft),
standing area of 0.25 m2 (2.69 ft2) and standing width at the standing position of at least 500 mm
(20 in.) per person. Seated positions are to have width of at least 450 mm (18 in.). Personnel are to
be positioned within the envelop of the top and bottom structures of the basket such that they are
protected during horizontal impact or loss of grip.
Commentary:
ABS REQUIREMENTS FOR CERTIFICATION OF LIFTING APPLIANCES • 2026 228

<!-- PDF page 235 -->

Chapter 7 Personnel Transfer Appliances
Section 1 Rigid Personnel Transfer Baskets 7-1
Additional guidance on dimensional sizing is provided in the ABS Guidance Notes on the Application of Ergonomics
to Marine Systems. When evaluating crew exposure to shock, guidance is provided in Appendix 4 of ABS Guide
for Crew Habitability of Ships.
End of Commentary
vii) For standing positions, safety lanyards and quick-release hooks are to be provided for attaching to
clothes/harnesses to stabilize the personnel during transfer. For seated arrangements, each seat is
to be equipped with a seat belt. All hooks and seat belts are to be easy to disconnect in case of
emergency egress. In addition, grab bars or handles are to be provided at the standing or seated
positions for personnel to hold themselves.
viii) A designated area with means of holding or lashing for items (such as equipment, tools, and baggage)
is to be provided. The location is not to obstruct the egress route.
ix) It is to be fitted with a permanently installed nameplate indicating the manufacturer’s name, serial
number, year it was built, SWL, maximum number of personnel that it is designed to carry, gross
weight of fully loaded basket, and the certification authority.
x) Taglines are to be provided for crews of both vessels or units engaged in personnel transfer operation
to control the movement of the basket during lowering or lifting close to the unit/vessel deck.
xi) The floor is to be of, or coated with, non-slip material. Drains or grated flooring are to be provided to
allow self-draining in case of rain or immersion. The ends of any hollow sections are to be sealed to
prevent the ingress of water. Alternatively, the manufacturer is to provide means of effective cleaning
and inspection, or is to design the sections to maintain the structural integrity for the service life.
xii) It is to have bright or highly distinctive colors for good visibility.
xiii) It is to maintain upright position when the vessel deck is inclined up to fifteen (15) degrees.
xiv) The design of the top of the basket structure is to prevent snagging any loose gear items. Any opening
in the roof is not to allow a falling object of diameter 20 mm (0.8 in.) to pass through. As a minimum,
the roof of the basket is to be able to withstand the impact of a steel ball weighing 7 kg, falling from
a height of 2 m (6.56 ft), without plastic deformations exceeding 50 mm (2 in.).
xv) In the event of emergency where it lands into the sea, buoyancy devices are to be provided to maintain
it afloat. The design of the buoyancy devices is to consider whether the personnel are expected to
remain in the basket during immersion. In case they are floated by their personal flotation devices,
the buoyancy devices of the basket may be designed for gross weight minus personnel weight. This
is to be clearly stated in the Operations and Maintenance Manual. It may be partially submerged and
need not be upright when immersed in water.
15 Prototype Tests
Testing of the wire ropes, and loose gear items are to be in accordance with Sections 2-4 and 2-5 respectively.
The following prototype tests are applicable to the assembled rigid personnel transfer basket and are to be
carried out in the presence of the ABS Surveyor.
15.1 Load Test (1 April 2025)
A proof load of two times the SWL is to be applied to the rigid personnel transfer basket including the lifting
set. It is to be hoisted up for at least five (5) minutes such that the proof load is distributed to each component.
There is to be no permanent deformation or damage observed upon removal of the load.
15.3 Horizontal Impact Test
The SWL is to be applied to the rigid personnel transfer basket. The fully loaded basket is to be hoisted and
is to be then subjected to a horizontal impact against a vertical flat surface at a speed of not less than 1 m/s
ABS REQUIREMENTS FOR CERTIFICATION OF LIFTING APPLIANCES • 2026 229

<!-- PDF page 236 -->

Chapter 7 Personnel Transfer Appliances
Section 1 Rigid Personnel Transfer Baskets 7-1
(2.22 mph). There is to be no permanent deformation or damage observed after impact that affects the load
carrying capability.
15.5 Vertical Impact Test
The SWL is to be applied to the rigid personnel transfer basket. The fully loaded basket is to be hoisted and
is to be then subjected to a vertical impact against a solid, horizontal and flat surface such as a steel deck
at a speed of not less than 1 m/s (2.22 mph). There is to be no permanent deformation or damage observed
after impact that affects the load carrying capability.
15.7 Buoyancy Test
Test loads are to be applied to the rigid personnel transfer basket to achieve the design weight to be floated by
the buoyancy devices as per 7-1/13.xv. The buoyancy of the test loads used is to be negative in fresh water.
The fully loaded basket is to be lowered into a test tank or pool. The basket is to float in the water (positive
buoyancy) in water for a period no less than 1 hour.
15.9 Seat Assembly or Standing Platform Load Test
The seat assembly including any shock absorber that may be installed, or the standing platform is to withstand
a proof load of two (2) times the personnel weight (180 kg, 397 lb) for at least five (5) minutes. There is to
be no permanent deformation or damage observed upon removal of the load.
17 Inspection, Testing and Certification
17.1 General
Each rigid personnel transfer basket requested to be unit certified by 7-1/1 is to:
i) Have its design approved by ABS; plans and data as required by 7-1/7 are to be submitted to ABS
for approval
ii) To be surveyed during its construction for compliance with the approved design and tested as per
7-1/15, to the satisfaction of the Surveyor
17.3 Shop Inspection and Testing
The following shop inspection and tests are to be witnessed by the Surveyor on all rigid personnel transfer
basket that are requested to be unit certified by ABS under 7-1/1.
17.3.1 Material Tests
i) Materials for the construction of rigid personnel transfer basket are to be in accordance with
the requirements of 7-1/9.
ii) The Surveyor is to have access to certificates of all material tests, loose gear items and other
components associated to the basket.
17.3.2 Welded Fabrication
i) All welded fabrication is to be conducted with qualified welding procedures, by qualified
welders and welding consumables appropriate for the process and to the satisfaction of the
Surveyor.
ii) Nondestructive testing (NDT) plan is to be submitted for review and acceptance by the
Surveyor, refer to Section 2-4-2 of the ABS Rules for Materials and Welding (Part 2).
17.3.3 Shop Tests (1 April 2025)
i) Upon completion of fabrication and assembly, each rigid personnel transfer basket is to be
subjected to testing in accordance with 7-1/15.1 at the manufacturer's facility, which are to
be witnessed and reported on by the attending Surveyor. This is provided that the specific
ABS REQUIREMENTS FOR CERTIFICATION OF LIFTING APPLIANCES • 2026 230

<!-- PDF page 237 -->

Chapter 7 Personnel Transfer Appliances
Section 1 Rigid Personnel Transfer Baskets 7-1
design prototype testing has been carried out, otherwise prototype tests as per 7-1/15 are
to be carried out.
ii) Manufacturer's test schedule is to be submitted to the Surveyor for review before the testing.
Upon satisfactory fabrication and testing at the manufacturer’s facility, the Surveyor may issue a report
confirming that the basket has been manufactured and tested in accordance with these requirements,
indicating the model and serial number, a description of the basket, and the date of issue. The finished product
is to be marked in accordance with 4-1-1/3.9 of the Marine Vessel Rules.
19 Basket Maintenance
The basket’s maintenance and inspection are the responsibility of the basket owner or operator and not within
the scope of ABS’s approval, certification or classification.
Commentary:
The basket owner or operator should establish a basket maintenance and inspection program based on the manufacturer's
recommendations.
End of Commentary
